205:# --------------------------------------------------------------------------------- parsing
206:
207:
208:def _fail(index: int, entry: object, problem: str) -> None:
209:    raise PlayingTimeFileError(
210:        f"playing-time entry #{index} is unusable ({problem}). The offending entry is: "
211:        f"{json.dumps(entry, default=str)}. Fix it in the file -- a bad entry is NOT skipped, "
212:        "because an availability judgement Marc made and this loader quietly dropped would be "
213:        "invisible on the board."
214:    )
215:
216:
217:def parse_overrides(payload: object) -> tuple[PlayingTimeOverride, ...]:
218:    """Validate and convert a loaded JSON payload (or raw JSON text) into overrides.
219:
220:    Accepts the dict form (``{"schema": 1, "overrides": [...]}``) or a bare list of entries.
221:
222:    Raises:
223:        PlayingTimeFileError: on any malformed entry, naming it. Never returns a partial list.
224:    """
225:    if isinstance(payload, (str, bytes)):
226:        try:
227:            payload = json.loads(payload)
228:        except json.JSONDecodeError as exc:
229:            raise PlayingTimeFileError(f"not valid JSON: {exc}") from exc
230:
231:    if isinstance(payload, Mapping):
232:        entries = payload.get("overrides")
233:        if entries is None:
234:            raise PlayingTimeFileError(
235:                "playing-time payload is an object with no 'overrides' key; expected "
236:                '{"schema": 1, "overrides": [...]} or a bare list of entries'
237:            )
238:        schema = payload.get("schema", SCHEMA_VERSION)
239:        if schema not in (None, SCHEMA_VERSION):
240:            raise PlayingTimeFileError(
241:                f"playing-time file declares schema {schema!r}, but this build understands "
242:                f"{SCHEMA_VERSION}. Refusing to guess at the difference."
243:            )
244:    elif isinstance(payload, Sequence):
245:        entries = payload
246:    else:
247:        raise PlayingTimeFileError(
248:            f"playing-time payload is a {type(payload).__name__}; expected an object or a list"
249:        )
250:
251:    if not isinstance(entries, Sequence) or isinstance(entries, (str, bytes)):
252:        raise PlayingTimeFileError("'overrides' must be a list")
253:
254:    out: list[PlayingTimeOverride] = []
255:    seen: dict[str, int] = {}
256:    for i, entry in enumerate(entries):
257:        if not isinstance(entry, Mapping):
258:            _fail(i, entry, f"not an object but a {type(entry).__name__}")
259:        missing = [f for f in REQUIRED_FIELDS if f not in entry]
260:        if missing:
261:            _fail(i, entry, f"missing required field(s) {missing}")
262:
263:        raw_pid = entry["player_id"]
264:        if raw_pid in (None, "", "null"):
265:            # decisions.py gives `null` a documented meaning (source-wide). Availability has no
266:            # such grain, so the same shape here is a mistake and must not be guessed at.
267:            _fail(
268:                i,
269:                entry,
270:                "player_id is null/empty -- unlike a projection decision, a playing-time "
271:                "override has no source-wide grain: availability is a fact about one player",
272:            )
273:        player_id = str(raw_pid).strip()
274:
275:        raw_games = entry["games"]
276:        if isinstance(raw_games, bool) or not isinstance(raw_games, (int, float)):
277:            _fail(i, entry, f"games must be a number, not a {type(raw_games).__name__}")
278:        games = float(raw_games)
279:        if not math.isfinite(games):
280:            _fail(i, entry, f"games is {raw_games!r}, which is not a real number")
281:        if games < 0:
282:            # The ONLY bound enforced here. No maximum is invented: the fitted availability
283:            # curve supplies the ceiling per player, and a second hardcoded one would be a
284:            # number nobody derived.
285:            _fail(i, entry, f"games is negative ({games}); a player cannot play fewer than 0")
286:
287:        reason = str(entry["reason"]).strip()
288:        date = str(entry["date"]).strip()
289:        if not reason:
290:            _fail(i, entry, "empty reason -- an override with no stated reason is not auditable")
291:        if not date:
292:            _fail(i, entry, "empty date")
293:
294:        if player_id in seen:
295:            # Not an error: a re-judgement is legitimate (PUP in August, activated in September).
296:            # Logged rather than silent, because two lines for one player in a hand-edited file
297:            # is also what a botched edit looks like.
298:            log.info(
299:                "playing-time entry #%d re-decides %s (previously entry #%d); the later entry "
300:                "wins",
301:                i,
302:                player_id,
303:                seen[player_id],
304:            )
305:        seen[player_id] = i
306:
307:        out.append(
308:            PlayingTimeOverride(
309:                player_id=player_id,
310:                games=games,
311:                reason=reason,
312:                date=date,
313:                player_name=str(entry.get("player_name") or "").strip(),
314:                designation=str(entry.get("designation") or "").strip(),
315:            )
316:        )
317:    return tuple(out)
318:
319:
320:def load_overrides(path: Path | None = None) -> tuple[PlayingTimeOverride, ...]:
321:    """Read ``data/playing_time.json``. A MISSING file means no overrides.
322:
323:    A missing file is the ordinary state and must never break a board build. Anything else --
324:    unreadable, malformed, or present-but-empty -- is NOT ordinary and raises
325:    :class:`PlayingTimeFileError`. Failing open here would quietly un-apply a human judgement,
326:    which is the one degradation this module must never do (the same rule, and the same reason,
327:    as ``decisions.load_decisions``).
328:    """
329:    p = Path(path) if path is not None else OVERRIDES_PATH
330:    if not p.exists():
331:        return ()
332:    try:
333:        text = p.read_text(encoding="utf-8")
334:    except OSError as exc:
335:        raise PlayingTimeFileError(f"cannot read {p}: {exc}") from exc
336:    if not text.strip():
337:        raise PlayingTimeFileError(
338:            f"{p} exists but is empty. An empty file is not the same as no overrides -- it is "
339:            "what a truncated write looks like. Delete the file to mean 'no overrides', or "
340:            "restore its contents."
341:        )
342:    try:
343:        return parse_overrides(text)
344:    except PlayingTimeFileError as exc:
345:        raise PlayingTimeFileError(f"{p}: {exc}") from exc
346:
347:
348:def merge_overrides(
349:    existing: Iterable[PlayingTimeOverride], incoming: Iterable[PlayingTimeOverride]
350:) -> tuple[PlayingTimeOverride, ...]:
351:    """``incoming`` wins on a shared ``player_id``; everything else keeps its original order."""
352:    by_key: dict[str, PlayingTimeOverride] = {}
353:    order: list[str] = []
354:    for o in (*existing, *incoming):
355:        if o.player_id not in by_key:
356:            order.append(o.player_id)
357:        by_key[o.player_id] = o
358:    return tuple(by_key[k] for k in order)
359:
360:
361:def save_overrides(overrides: Iterable[PlayingTimeOverride], path: Path | None = None) -> Path:
362:    """Write the file atomically (temp + ``os.replace``), the way this repo writes any state."""
363:    p = Path(path) if path is not None else OVERRIDES_PATH
364:    p.parent.mkdir(parents=True, exist_ok=True)
365:    payload = {
366:        "schema": SCHEMA_VERSION,
367:        "_note": _FILE_NOTE,
368:        "overrides": [o.as_json() for o in overrides],
369:    }
370:    tmp = p.with_suffix(p.suffix + ".tmp")
371:    tmp.write_text(json.dumps(payload, indent=2) + "\n", encoding="utf-8")
372:    os.replace(tmp, p)
373:    return p
374:
375:
376:# ------------------------------------------------------------------ the board's input
377:
378:
379:def overrides_by_pid(
380:    overrides: Iterable[PlayingTimeOverride],
381:) -> dict[str, PlayingTimeOverride]:
382:    """``player_id -> override``, later entries winning. What the board build looks up."""
383:    out: dict[str, PlayingTimeOverride] = {}
384:    for o in overrides:
385:        out[o.player_id] = o
386:    return out

388:
389:@dataclass(frozen=True)
390:class Binding:
391:    """What an override actually did to one player, once the curve had its say."""
392:
393:    override: PlayingTimeOverride
394:    #: The games figure the pipeline would have used with no override at all. ``None`` when the
395:    #: active source published none (so the fitted prior would have supplied the volume).
396:    was: float | None
397:    #: The games figure in force now: ``min(override.games, curve)``.
398:    now: float
399:    #: The curve value that clamped it, carried so a badge can explain an upward clamp.
400:    curve: float
401:
402:    @property
403:    def clamped(self) -> bool:
404:        """Did the availability curve cut the override down?"""
405:        return self.override.games > self.curve + 1e-9
406:
407:    @property
408:    def moved(self) -> bool:
409:        """Did this override actually CHANGE the number the board uses?
410:
411:        ``was is None`` counts as moved: replacing "the fitted prior decides" with an explicit
412:        figure is a real change, and it is the FantasyPros/FantasySharks case.
413:        """
414:        return self.was is None or abs(self.now - self.was) > 1e-9
415:
416:    def describe(self) -> str:
417:        before = "curve prior" if self.was is None else f"{self.was:.2f}"
418:        tail = (
419:            f" (clamped from {self.override.games:.2f} by the {self.curve:.2f} healthy-rank "
420:            f"curve)"
421:            if self.clamped
422:            else ""
423:        )
424:        return f"{self.override.describe()} -- games {before} -> {self.now:.2f}{tail}"
425:
426:

429:
430:    Args:

1:"""Player pool for the live server: search, tier board, and roster tracking.
2:
3:This module is deliberately independent of whatever `draftroom.draft.recommend` needs
4:internally for real draft-value math (EVoB, replacement levels, VONA) -- that engine is being
5:built concurrently and this file must not depend on it. What the live UI needs *right now* is
6:much smaller: every player's identity (id/name/pos/team/bye) and an ordering to rank on.
7:
8:The pool has TWO TIERS, and the distinction is the whole point of this module:
9:
10:* **Ranked** players -- present in the cached FFC 2QB ADP payload (~189). These carry a real
11:  ADP, a std_dev the survival model needs, and an ADP-derived placeholder `value`.
12:* **Unranked** players -- every other active skill-position player on an NFL roster, taken from
13:  the cached Sleeper universe (~949 total). No ADP, no projection, `value` 0.0, and they are
14:  never recommended. They exist so the board can RECORD them.
15:
16:Why both: this tool's first job is bookkeeping. A 10-team x 15-round draft is 150 picks, and a
17:pool of only 189 ranked players leaves 39 of margin, so by the late rounds the room is taking
18:players the board cannot see and every one becomes a manual write-in. Marc asked for every
19:draftable name to be listed "even if we don't have projections" -- tracking needs the name,
20:recommending needs the projection, and those are different requirements.
21:
22:Bye weeks are a property of the TEAM, not the player, so the 32-team bye map is derived from
23:whichever FFC rows carry one and applied to the whole universe (Sleeper's own payload carries
24:`bye_week` for 0 of 988 records -- verified 2026-08-17).

121:@dataclass(frozen=True)
122:class PoolPlayer:
123:    """One player, reduced to what the live server needs to render and search on."""
124:
125:    player_id: str
126:    name: str
127:    pos: str
128:    team: str
129:    bye: int | None
130:    adp: float
131:    stdev: float
132:    overall_rank: int  # 1 = best ADP, i.e. most valuable available
133:    #: The real DraftValue from the validated board when the join landed (value_is_real=True,
134:    #: see REAL_VALUE_NOTE); otherwise the ADP placeholder in fallback mode, or 0.0 for a
135:    #: ranked player the board build excluded (name kept for bookkeeping, no evaluation).
136:    value: float
137:    #: True when this player came from the ADP feed and so has a real ADP/std_dev/value.
138:    #: False for roster-only players who exist to be RECORDED, never recommended. The UI must
139:    #: show these as "no projection" rather than implying a value of zero is an evaluation.
140:    is_ranked: bool = True
141:    #: Cross-source (Sleeper/FantasyPros/ESPN/FantasySharks) PPG sigma from
142:    #: draftroom.valuation.disagreement,
143:    #: joined in by name+team -- None when <2 independent sources resolved (never a fabricated
144:    #: 0.0). Best-effort: absent entirely if the cached prep data needed to compute it is
145:    #: missing (see `_load_disagreement_by_key`).
146:    sigma_ppg: float | None = None
147:    #: Coefficient of variation (points_stdev / points_mean) across those same sources. None
148:    #: under the same conditions as sigma_ppg.
149:    disagreement_cv: float | None = None
150:    #: True when disagreement_cv >= DISAGREEMENT_CV_THRESHOLD. A danger signal only -- LOW/False
151:    #: here is NOT evidence the projection is accurate (see valuation/disagreement.py's caveat).
152:    disagreement_high: bool = False
153:    #: Sleeper's own injury/practice-report fields, from the cached universe file. None when
154:    #: Sleeper has nothing on record (the normal case for a healthy player).
155:    injury_status: str | None = None
156:    practice_participation: str | None = None
157:    depth_chart_order: int | None = None
158:    #: Season-total SD of `value` (from cross-source disagreement, via the real board). 0.0
159:    #: when unknown -- absence of spread data, never "certainty". (Appended at the end of the
160:    #: field list deliberately: tests construct PoolPlayer positionally up to earlier fields.)
161:    value_sd: float = 0.0
162:    #: True only when `value` came from the validated real board. False for the ADP
163:    #: placeholder, for excluded-ranked players, and for all unranked players.
164:    value_is_real: bool = False
165:    #: source key -> that source's own league-scored **SEASON POINTS** for this player (same
166:    #: scoring and bonus model the board uses), carrying a "blend" entry alongside "sleeper",
167:    #: "espn", "fantasypros" and "fantasysharks" so the UI can show all five side by side with
168:    #: no extra fetch.
169:    #: SEASON POINTS, deliberately -- not DraftValue: dv depends on the whole pool's replacement
170:    #: level, so a per-source dv would not be comparable row to row, whereas season points is
171:    #: the projection disagreement itself, in the open. A source with no data for the player
172:    #: simply has no key (never a fabricated 0.0); None means no real board was joined at all.
173:    #: (Appended at the END of the field list deliberately: tests construct PoolPlayer
174:    #: positionally through the earlier fields.)
175:    value_by_source: dict[str, float] | None = None
176:    #: Marc's adjudicated rejections that actually APPLIED to this player, as plain dicts
177:    #: ready for the payload (see docs/REVIEW_QUEUE.md). None = nothing was rejected for
178:    #: him. A rejection must ALWAYS be visible on the board -- a value silently different
179:    #: from what the sources imply is exactly what this field exists to prevent.
180:    projection_decisions: tuple[dict[str, str], ...] | None = None
181:    #: Marc's manual playing-time override for this player, when one actually MOVED his
182:    #: expected games (draftroom.valuation.playing_time). None = no override changed anything
183:    #: for him. Like `projection_decisions`, this must ALWAYS be visible on the board: an
184:    #: expected-games figure that came from a human and looks like a model output is precisely
185:    #: the confusion this field exists to prevent. Informational for rendering only -- the
186:    #: number itself was already applied upstream, in the board build.
187:    playing_time: dict[str, object] | None = None

195:def available_sources() -> list[dict[str, Any]]:
196:    """One entry per source: ``{key, label, player_count, total_count, note, available}``.
197:
198:    ``player_count`` is the number of ADP-ranked players this source could actually VALUE (a
199:    real board value joined on), which is the number that answers "how much of the board does
200:    this source cover". ``total_count`` is the whole pool including the roster-only tier that
201:    exists purely so the board can record a name (CLAUDE.md: "The player pool is TWO TIERS").
202:
203:    Never raises, and safe to call at startup: a source that cannot build is reported with
204:    ``player_count`` 0, ``available`` False, and a note carrying the failure, so the UI can grey
205:    it out instead of the whole control disappearing.
206:    """
207:    global _SOURCES_CACHE
208:    if _SOURCES_CACHE is not None:
209:        return [dict(entry) for entry in _SOURCES_CACHE]
210:
211:    out: list[dict[str, Any]] = []
212:    for key in SOURCE_KEYS:
213:        note = SOURCE_NOTES.get(key, "")
214:        try:
215:            pool = pool_for_source(key)
216:        except Exception as exc:  # noqa: BLE001 - a broken source must not break the toggle
217:            log.warning("projection source %r unavailable: %s: %s", key, type(exc).__name__, exc)
218:            out.append(
219:                {
220:                    "key": key,
221:                    "label": SOURCE_LABELS.get(key, key),
222:                    "player_count": 0,
223:                    "total_count": 0,
224:                    "available": False,
225:                    "note": f"UNAVAILABLE ({type(exc).__name__}: {exc}). {note}",
226:                }
227:            )
228:            continue
229:
230:        valued = real_values_in(pool)
231:        ranked = sum(1 for p in pool if p.is_ranked)
232:        available = valued > 0
233:        detail = note
234:        if not available:
235:            detail = (
236:                "UNAVAILABLE (built, but valued 0 of "
237:                f"{ranked} ranked players -- the real board did not join, so this pool is in "
238:                f"ADP-placeholder fallback mode). {note}"
239:            )
240:        elif valued < ranked:
241:            detail = (
242:                f"{note} Valued {valued} of {ranked} ranked players; the rest are listed for "
243:                "bookkeeping with no projection."
244:            )
245:        out.append(
246:            {
247:                "key": key,
248:                "label": SOURCE_LABELS.get(key, key),
249:                "player_count": valued,
250:                "total_count": len(pool),
251:                "available": available,
252:                "note": detail,
253:            }
254:        )
255:
256:    _SOURCES_CACHE = out
257:    return [dict(entry) for entry in out]

613:def _build_board_players(pool: list[live_data.PoolPlayer]) -> list[Any] | None:
614:    """Convert the live pool to `recommend.BoardPlayer`, if that module is importable.
615:
616:    As of 2026-08-18 the pool's `value`/`value_sd` ARE the real risk-adjusted DraftValues from
617:    the validated board (`live_data` joins `validate.board.build_real_board()` at load time --
618:    a Codex review caught draft night serving an ADP placeholder the sims never exercised).
619:    In fallback mode (no cached board) `value` degrades to the ADP placeholder, loudly flagged
620:    in the payload as `board_source: "placeholder"`. `is_ranked` passes through so the engine
621:    keeps roster-only write-ins in its need math without ever recommending them.
622:    """
623:    if _recommend_mod is None:
624:        return None
625:    try:
626:        return [
627:            _recommend_mod.BoardPlayer(
628:                player_id=p.player_id,
629:                name=p.name,
630:                pos=p.pos,
631:                team=p.team,
632:                bye=p.bye,
633:                adp=p.adp,
634:                stdev=p.stdev,
635:                dv=p.value,
636:                dv_sd=p.value_sd,
637:                is_ranked=p.is_ranked,
638:            )
639:            for p in pool
640:        ]
641:    except Exception as exc:  # noqa: BLE001 - defensive, see module docstring
642:        log.warning("could not build BoardPlayer list for recommend.recommend(): %s", exc)

665:
666:    `recommend.recommend(state, cfg, players)` always answers for whoever is on the clock
667:    *right now* (`state.current_pick`) -- it has no pick-number parameter, and itself returns
668:    an explicit "Not on the clock" placeholder when `state.is_my_pick` is False. So there is no
669:    real per-pick target to forward here; `target=mine` vs `target=clock` is purely a client-
670:    side label until it's my turn, at which point they agree. Any failure at all (missing
671:    module, a partially-built one, an unexpected exception mid-computation) falls back to our
672:    own placeholder rather than a 500 -- see module docstring.
673:
674:    `elite_qb_rank_cutoff` is fix "C"(b)'s visible knob (CLAUDE.md/task spec): the UI exposes it
675:    as a control rather than hardcoding the spec default, so Marc can dial it to 0 (off) or wider

 succeeded in 397ms:
1:"""EVoB -- expected value over baseline, the per-game valuation this model drafts on.
2:
3:``EVoB = (PPG - baseline_PPG) * expected_games``
4:
5:The alternative (season-total VBD, "projected points minus the baseline's projected points")
6:gets the ordering wrong whenever games played differ, which is most of the time. Harstad's
7:example, kept as the first test in the suite: a player who scores 83.2 points in 7 games is
8:worth more than one who scores 84.2 in 16, because in the ten weeks he plays he is beating
9:replacement by six points a game, and the weeks he misses are covered by a bench player who is
10:roughly replacement anyway. Season totals rank them backwards.
11:
12::class:`DraftValue` keeps the components rather than collapsing to one number, because the UI
13:has to explain a pick in two or three bullets each backed by a computed number (CLAUDE.md).
14:"""
15:
16:from __future__ import annotations
17:
18:from dataclasses import dataclass
19:from typing import Any, Iterable, Mapping
20:
21:from draftroom.config import LeagueConfig
22:from draftroom.valuation.replacement import (
23:    EXPECTED_GAMES_CURVE,
24:    AvailabilityBin,
25:    ReplacementInfo,
26:    replacement_levels,
27:    resolve_players,
28:)
29:
30:__all__ = ["DraftValue", "evob", "compute_draft_values"]
31:
32:
33:def evob(ppg: float, baseline_ppg: float, expected_games: float) -> float:
34:    """Expected points above replacement over the season the player is expected to play."""
35:    return (float(ppg) - float(baseline_ppg)) * float(expected_games)
36:
37:
38:@dataclass(frozen=True)
39:class DraftValue:
40:    """One player's draft value with every input kept visible.
41:
42:    ``dv = evob - lam * sigma_season`` is the risk-adjusted number. ``lam`` is a *preference*,
43:    not an estimate: at 0 the model is risk-neutral and ``dv == evob``.
44:    """
45:
46:    player_id: str
47:    pos: str
48:    ppg: float
49:    baseline_ppg: float
50:    expected_games: float
51:    evob: float
52:    sigma_season: float
53:    lam: float
54:    dv: float
55:    name: str = ""
56:    #: How ``sigma_season`` was obtained -- "given", "from_sigma_ppg", or "absent".
57:    sigma_source: str = "absent"
58:
59:    @property
60:    def points_above_baseline_per_game(self) -> float:
61:        return self.ppg - self.baseline_ppg
62:
63:
64:def _sigma_season_for(player: Any, games: float) -> tuple[float, str]:
65:    """Resolve a player's season-total sigma.
66:
67:    UNVERIFIED MODELING ASSUMPTION: when only a PPG sigma is available, season sigma is taken
68:    as ``sigma_ppg * expected_games`` (perfectly correlated weeks) rather than
69:    ``sigma_ppg * sqrt(expected_games)`` (independent weeks). The dominant uncertainty in a
70:    season projection is talent/role/usage, which persists week to week, so the linear form is
71:    the conservative one -- but this has not been fit to data. Supplying ``sigma_season``
72:    directly bypasses it entirely, and the default of 0.0 means the risk penalty stays inert
73:    until someone deliberately turns it on.
74:    """
75:    if getattr(player, "sigma_season", None) is not None:
76:        return float(player.sigma_season), "given"
77:    sigma_ppg = getattr(player, "sigma_ppg", None)
78:    if sigma_ppg is not None:
79:        return float(sigma_ppg) * float(games), "from_sigma_ppg"
80:    return 0.0, "absent"
81:
82:
83:def compute_draft_values(
84:    players: Iterable[Any],
85:    cfg: LeagueConfig,
86:    lam: float = 0.0,
87:    *,
88:    curves: Mapping[str, tuple[AvailabilityBin, ...]] = EXPECTED_GAMES_CURVE,
89:    replacement: Mapping[str, ReplacementInfo] | None = None,
90:) -> dict[str, DraftValue]:
91:    """Draft value for every player, against replacement levels derived from ``cfg``.
92:
93:    Args:
94:        players: player-ish records (see
95:            :func:`~draftroom.valuation.replacement.resolve_players`).
96:        cfg: the league. Every baseline comes from it; nothing is hardcoded.
97:        lam: risk aversion. ``dv = evob - lam * sigma_season``. 0 = risk-neutral.
98:        curves: rank-conditional expected-games curves, see
99:            :data:`~draftroom.valuation.replacement.EXPECTED_GAMES_CURVE`.
100:        replacement: precomputed baselines, to avoid recomputing during a live draft when the
101:            pool has not changed. Computed from ``players`` when omitted.
102:
103:    Raises:
104:        KeyError: a player sits at a position with no replacement level -- meaning the league
105:            has no lineup demand for it. Scoring such a player against an invented baseline
106:            would be a silently wrong number, so it raises instead.
107:    """
108:    resolved = resolve_players(players, cfg, curves=curves)
109:    levels = (
110:        replacement_levels(resolved, cfg, curves=curves) if replacement is None else replacement
111:    )
112:
113:    out: dict[str, DraftValue] = {}
114:    for p in resolved:
115:        info = levels.get(p.pos)
116:        if info is None:
117:            raise KeyError(
118:                f"player {p.player_id!r} is at position {p.pos!r}, which has no replacement "
119:                f"level in this league (positions with lineup demand: {sorted(levels)}). "
120:                f"Filter the pool to rostered positions before valuing it."
121:            )
122:        games = float(p.expected_games or 0.0)
123:        sigma_season, sigma_source = _sigma_season_for(p, games)
124:        value = evob(p.ppg, info.baseline_ppg, games)
125:        out[p.player_id] = DraftValue(
126:            player_id=p.player_id,
127:            pos=p.pos,
128:            ppg=p.ppg,
129:            baseline_ppg=info.baseline_ppg,
130:            expected_games=games,
131:            evob=value,
132:            sigma_season=sigma_season,
133:            lam=float(lam),
134:            dv=value - float(lam) * sigma_season,
135:            name=p.name,
136:            sigma_source=sigma_source,
137:        )
138:    return out

376:# ================================================= 7: real-board expected-games cap respected

378:
379:def check_expected_games_capped_by_curve(
380:    players: Sequence[PlayerSeason], cfg: LeagueConfig
381:) -> CheckResult:
382:    """Every REAL-board season's ``expected_games`` must respect the availability curve.
383:
384:    Added 2026-08-18 after a Codex review caught exactly the failure this detects: the board
385:    build passed Sleeper's per-player games straight through as an explicit override, which made
386:    the rank-conditional ``EXPECTED_GAMES_CURVE`` (that same day's headline projection fix)
387:    silently inert on the real board -- and the then-existing games check couldn't see it,
388:    because it ran only against a synthetic pool. This check runs against whatever REAL seasons
389:    the gate was handed: for each player carrying an explicit ``expected_games``, recompute the
390:    curve value at their PPG rank within position and require ``expected_games <= curve`` (the
391:    build's documented ``min(source, curve)`` policy), plus at least one player where the cap
392:    is exactly binding -- proof the cap code actually executed rather than the whole board
393:    happening to sit under the curve.
394:
395:    Players with ``expected_games is None`` are skipped (they go through the curve downstream
396:    by construction); a pool with NO explicit games at all (the synthetic monotonicity pools)
397:    passes vacuously with that stated.
398:    """
399:    from draftroom.valuation.replacement import expected_games as _curve_games
400:
401:    explicit = [p for p in players if getattr(p, "expected_games", None) is not None]
402:    if not explicit:
403:        return CheckResult(
404:            "expected_games_capped_by_curve",
405:            True,
406:            "no player in this pool carries an explicit expected_games (all use the curve "
407:            "downstream by construction) -- nothing to cap, passing vacuously",
408:        )
409:
410:    by_pos: dict[str, list[PlayerSeason]] = {}
411:    for p in players:
412:        by_pos.setdefault(p.pos, []).append(p)
413:
414:    tol = 1e-9
415:    offenders: list[str] = []
416:    binding = 0
417:    for pos, group in by_pos.items():
418:        for rank, p in enumerate(sorted(group, key=lambda x: -x.ppg), start=1):
419:            if p.expected_games is None:
420:                continue
421:            cap = _curve_games(pos, rank=rank, weeks=cfg.weeks)
422:            if float(p.expected_games) > cap + tol:
423:                offenders.append(
424:                    f"{p.name or p.player_id}({pos} rank {rank}): {p.expected_games:.2f} > cap {cap:.2f}"
425:                )
426:            elif abs(float(p.expected_games) - cap) <= tol:
427:                binding += 1
428:
429:    passed = not offenders and binding >= 1
430:    detail = (
431:        f"checked {len(explicit)} players with explicit expected_games against the "
432:        f"rank-conditional curve; cap exactly binding for {binding} of them"
433:    )
434:    if offenders:
435:        detail += f" | FAIL: {len(offenders)} above the cap: {'; '.join(offenders[:6])}"
436:    elif binding < 1:
437:        detail += (
438:            " | FAIL: the cap never bound for a single player -- either every source projects "
439:            "under the fitted availability curve everywhere (implausible) or the min(source, "
440:            "curve) cap is no longer being applied in the board build"
441:        )
442:    return CheckResult("expected_games_capped_by_curve", passed, detail)

 succeeded in 403ms:
1:# Playing-time overrides
2:
3:The one place a human can set a player's expected games. Built 2026-08-24 to close the gap
4:`injury_vs_expected_games` could only complain about.
5:
6:## The gap this closes
7:
8:`injury_status` was carried on `PoolPlayer` and rendered as a badge, and it touched **nothing**
9:in the valuation. So whether a will-not-play designation reached `expected_games` depended
10:entirely on whether ESPN happened to price it in, which is accidental rather than principled.
11:Measured on the ranked pool 2026-08-20:
12:
13:| Player | Designation | ADP | ESPN games | Board credited | Healthy-rank curve |
14:|---|---|---|---|---|---|
15:| Alec Pierce (WR IND) | PUP | 70.3 | **17.0** | **15.50** | 15.50 (WR30) |
16:| Brandon Aiyuk (WR SF) | DNR | 148.7 | — | **8.46** | 8.46 (WR78) |
17:| George Kittle (TE SF) | PUP | — | 15.0 | discounted | — |
18:| Zach Charbonnet (RB SEA) | PUP | — | 11.0 | discounted | — |
19:
20:Kittle and Charbonnet came out right. Pierce and Aiyuk got the figure for a player about whom
21:nothing player-specific is known, at ADPs where that matters. Ricky Pearsall (IR) came out right
22:only by luck: he is off the board because Sleeper happened to zero his stat line, not because
23:anything read his status.
24:
25:The review queue could see all of this and could do nothing about it, which is why those rows are
26:marked `actionable: false`. Its only lever is `blend_statlines(rejected=...)`, and **rejecting a
27:source cannot change an availability figure**. No source is even at fault: ESPN's 17.0 is an
28:ordinary if-healthy projection, and "projections are not expectations" is a measured finding in
29:this repo. The missing lever was a playing-time file. This is it.
30:
31:## The file
32:
33:`data/playing_time.json`. Modelled on `data/projection_decisions.json`: checked first, permanent,
34:auditable, hand-editable without running anything.
35:
36:```json
37:{
38:  "schema": 1,
39:  "overrides": [
40:    {"player_id": "8142", "player_name": "Alec Pierce", "games": 11.0,
41:     "designation": "PUP",
42:     "reason": "not expected back before ~week 5",
43:     "date": "2026-08-24"}
44:  ]
45:}
46:```
47:
48:Required: `player_id`, `games`, `reason`, `date`. Optional: `player_name` (human-facing only,
49:never used for matching) and `designation` (audit trail, never affects the number). A bare list
50:is accepted too, because that is what a hand-edit eventually looks like.
51:
52:**`player_id` is never null.** `decisions.py` gives `null` a documented meaning — the decision
53:applies source-wide. Availability has no such grain: it is a fact about one player. The same
54:shape here is a mistake, so it is refused rather than reinterpreted.
55:
56:## The rule
57:
58:```
59:expected_games = min(the human's figure, curve(pos, rank-by-ppg))
60:```
61:
62:The override **replaces** whatever games figure the active source published — including the
63:`None` that FantasyPros and FantasySharks leave behind, which makes an override the only thing
64:that can turn an implicit "let the fitted prior decide" into an explicit number. The same
65:rank-conditional availability curve that already caps every other player then clamps it.
66:
67:The clamp is load-bearing in both directions, and it is derived rather than chosen:
68:
69:- **Downward passes straight through.** Bad news is the point. An override of 11 for a PUP player
70:  lands at 11, because the curve's 15.50 was never a claim about *him*.
71:- **Upward stops at the curve.** The curve is fitted actual availability at that positional rank.
72:  You can say "he is fully cleared, ignore the source's 11" and get him back to the healthy-rank
73:  figure. You cannot push him past it, because that is claiming better-than-typical durability
74:  for a rank on the strength of a press report — and it is the one direction whose error is
75:  expensive, since it inflates a player you would then draft at full value.
76:
77:The clamp is also why this feature **weakens no gate to admit itself**.
78:`check_expected_games_capped_by_curve` stays true by construction rather than by exemption. An
79:override mechanism that had to loosen an invariant would be indistinguishable from the bug that
80:invariant exists to catch. Pinned in `tests/test_playing_time_wiring.py`.
81:
82:**PPG is never touched.** This moves the games VOLUME a per-game rate is credited for, exactly as
83:the curve cap already did. A view that a player will be *worse per game* is a projection question
84:and belongs in the review queue.
85:
86:## No threshold is invented, here or anywhere
87:
88:Nothing in this repo asserts what a PUP/IR/DNR designation costs in games, and this file does not
89:change that. The reason is in `candidates.NO_EMPIRICAL_DESIGNATION_FIT`: Sleeper's designation is
90:current-year while the only per-player games history in the cache is 2025 actuals, so **no
91:games-missed figure is derivable from this repo's data**. Asserting one would be exactly the
92:arbitrary rule the null-test standard exists to stop.
93:
94:So the loader validates only that `games` is a non-negative real number. It deliberately enforces
95:**no maximum** — the fitted curve supplies the ceiling per player, and a second hardcoded ceiling
96:would be a number nobody derived.
97:
98:## Fails closed
99:
100:Same asymmetry as `decisions.load_decisions`, for the same reason:
101:
102:- **Missing file** → no overrides. The ordinary state; the board builds normally.
103:- **Present but empty, or malformed** → `PlayingTimeFileError`, naming the offending entry, and
104:  it propagates through the board build uncaught.
105:
106:Every other optional input in `validate/board.py` degrades to "this source contributes nothing",
107:because a missing FantasyPros CSV has nothing to do with whether the board is sound. A bad
108:overrides file is the opposite: degrading would silently stop applying a judgement about a player
109:Marc knows something about, and the board would look fine while ignoring him.
110:
111:## What you see on the board
112:
113:A `NN.NG` badge next to the player's name, in the accent colour rather than REJ's red — a
114:different kind of decision, so a different badge. The tooltip states the reason, the date, what
115:the pipeline would have used, and whether the curve clamped the figure.
116:
117:**Only overrides that actually MOVED a number are badged.** Same rule as REJ. An override the
118:curve clamped back to the figure the board already had changed nothing, and a badge on it would
119:point at a decision that did nothing. Those are still loaded, still on file, and logged at
120:WARNING by the board build — an inert override is usually a sign the note is not doing what its
121:author thinks.
122:
123:The `was` figure behind that comparison is the **already-capped counterfactual**, not the raw
124:source number. Getting this wrong badged every override on a player the curve had already cut
125:down: Josh Allen's source figure is 17.0 against a 16.6 curve, so an absurd override clamped to
126:16.6 read as "17.0 → 16.6" and looked like a change it did not make. Caught by its own test
127:during the build.
128:
129:## An overridden player leaves the review queue
130:
131:Once a player has an override that moved his number, `injury_vs_expected_games` stops firing for
132:him. The gap it exists to surface is closed for that player: the board's figure is now a human
133:judgement rather than an unexamined default, which is the only thing the check was complaining
134:about. Handing the decision back as a fresh candidate would be noise, and the `hygiene` wording
135:("a source did price in N games") would be actively false — no source did.
136:
137:The disappearance is **reported, not silent**: those players land in
138:`ReviewQueue.settled_by_override`, with a note in the queue and a line in the HTML page. A row
139:that vanished because the question was answered must not look like a detector that stopped
140:working.
141:
142:## Where it lives
143:
144:| Path | Role |
145:|---|---|
146:| `backend/draftroom/valuation/playing_time.py` | the file format, the fail-closed loader, `bind()` |
147:| `backend/draftroom/validate/board.py` | loads it; applies it inside `_cap_expected_games_by_curve` |
148:| `backend/draftroom/valuation/candidates.py` | `ImpactEngine` carries it through counterfactuals; the injury detector defers to it |
149:| `backend/draftroom/live_data.py` | `_playing_time_payload` → `PoolPlayer.playing_time` |
150:| `backend/draftroom/server.py` | the board row's `playing_time` key |
151:| `frontend/src/components/TierBoard.tsx` | `PlayingTimeBadge` |
152:| `tests/test_playing_time.py` | the file and the clamp rule in isolation (39 tests) |
153:| `tests/test_playing_time_wiring.py` | the real cached board, end to end (11 tests) |
154:
155:`ImpactEngine` needs the overrides in both the baseline and every counterfactual. The baseline
156:seasons already carry the applied figure (re-applying is idempotent, since the override is
157:already curve-clamped), but `_rebuild_season` derives `expected_games` from the statline and
158:would otherwise hand an overridden player his source's figure back mid-recomputation, making the
159:review queue's impact column wrong for exactly the players a human has looked at.

1212:def _announce_existing_draft(board: DraftBoard) -> None:
1213:    """Say loudly, at startup, that the event log already contains picks.
1214:
1215:    A non-empty log is LEGITIMATE -- it is exactly what a crash-recovery relaunch looks like, and
1216:    replaying it is the whole point of the append-only design -- so this never refuses to start.
1217:    But it is also what a stale log looks like, and the two are indistinguishable from the outside.
1218:
1219:    This exists because it actually happened: on 2026-08-20 the live log still held four
1220:    smoke-test picks from two days earlier. Launching on that would have opened the draft at pick
1221:    5 with Josh Allen, Jaxson Dart, Sam Darnold and C.J. Stroud already off the board, and the
1222:    only clue on screen would have been a board that looked subtly wrong in a room full of people.
1223:    Silence was the bug; a loud, specific summary is the fix.
1224:    """
1225:    st = board.state
1226:    filled = [p for p in sorted(st.picks.values(), key=lambda x: x.pick_no) if p.is_filled]
1227:    if not filled:
1228:        log.info("Draft log is empty -- starting a fresh draft at pick 1.")
1229:        return
1230:
1231:    recent = ", ".join(
1232:        f"{p.pick_no}:{(board.pool_by_id[p.player_id].name if p.player_id in board.pool_by_id else p.player_id) if p.player_id else p.stub_name}"
1233:        for p in filled[-4:]
1234:    )
1235:    log.warning(
1236:        "\n"
1237:        "  ============================================================\n"
1238:        "  RESUMING AN EXISTING DRAFT -- the log already has %d pick(s).\n"
1239:        "  Clock opens at pick %s. Most recent: %s\n"
1240:        "\n"
1241:        "  If this IS your draft in progress, carry on -- this is crash recovery working.\n"
1242:        "  If you expected a FRESH board, stop now and archive the log:\n"
1243:        "      move %s %s.archived\n"
1244:        "  Deleting picks is never necessary; the log is append-only by design.\n"
1245:        "  ============================================================",

395:    #: active source published none (so the fitted prior would have supplied the volume).
396:    was: float | None
397:    #: The games figure in force now: ``min(override.games, curve)``.
398:    now: float
399:    #: The curve value that clamped it, carried so a badge can explain an upward clamp.
400:    curve: float
401:
402:    @property
403:    def clamped(self) -> bool:
404:        """Did the availability curve cut the override down?"""
405:        return self.override.games > self.curve + 1e-9
406:
407:    @property
408:    def moved(self) -> bool:
409:        """Did this override actually CHANGE the number the board uses?
410:
411:        ``was is None`` counts as moved: replacing "the fitted prior decides" with an explicit
412:        figure is a real change, and it is the FantasyPros/FantasySharks case.
413:        """
414:        return self.was is None or abs(self.now - self.was) > 1e-9

420:            f"curve)"
421:            if self.clamped
422:            else ""
423:        )
424:        return f"{self.override.describe()} -- games {before} -> {self.now:.2f}{tail}"

254:def test_a_malformed_overrides_file_fails_the_board_build(monkeypatch, tmp_path):
255:    """Deliberately NOT caught in the board build, exactly like DecisionsFileError.
256:
257:    Every other optional input here degrades to "this source contributes nothing", because a
258:    missing FantasyPros CSV has nothing to do with whether the board is sound. A bad overrides
259:    file is the opposite: degrading would silently stop applying a judgement Marc made, and the
260:    board would look fine while ignoring him.
261:    """
262:    from draftroom.valuation import playing_time as pt_mod
263:    from draftroom.validate import board as board_mod

268:    monkeypatch.setattr(board_mod, "load_overrides", lambda: load_overrides(p))

Completed the static trace of the board, live server, review queue, parser, call sites, and positional constructors. Focused test execution was blocked because the checked-in venv points to a missing Python 3.12 installation in this review environment.

  ✓ Trace callers, constructors, override semantics, and review-queue counterfactuals
  ✓ Run focused tests/static checks and report severity-ranked findings
codex
Do not ship as-is: one blocker allows draft mode to start with placeholder valuations after an override-file failure.

## Blocker

1. **`PlayingTimeFileError` is swallowed by the live-board fallback.**  
   [live_data.py](/C:/dev/draftroom/backend/draftroom/live_data.py:255) re-raises only `DecisionsFileError`; the broad handler immediately below converts every other exception into `{}`. [server.py](/C:/dev/draftroom/backend/draftroom/server.py:873) then starts normally with ADP-placeholder values.

   Failure scenario: `data/playing_time.json` is truncated before the draft. `build_real_board()` raises correctly, but draft mode boots, `/healthz` returns 200, and recommendations run on untrustworthy placeholder values. This violates the stated fail-closed contract.

   Fix: import `PlayingTimeFileError` in `live_data.py` and re-raise both decision-file exceptions. Add a regression test through `load_player_pool()` or `create_app()`; the current test stops at `build_real_board()`.

## Should-fix

2. **The `expected_games=None` counterfactual produces false “moved” badges.**  
   [board.py](/C:/dev/draftroom/backend/draftroom/validate/board.py:583) passes `None` as `Binding.was`, and [playing_time.py](/C:/dev/draftroom/backend/draftroom/valuation/playing_time.py:408) therefore declares every such override moved.

   Failure scenario: on FantasyPros, the prior would produce 15.5 games. An override of 99 is clamped to 15.5. EVoB is byte-identical, but the player is badged and removed from the injury queue as though his number changed.

   The numeric no-override counterfactual is `curve`, not `None`. Preserve “source supplied no games” in a separate field if needed for the tooltip. `games=0` is handled correctly.

3. **Any prior override can hide a new or unrelated injury designation.**  
   [candidates.py](/C:/dev/draftroom/backend/draftroom/valuation/candidates.py:1364) treats every applied override as settling the current injury finding, without checking the override’s stored designation. [candidates.py](/C:/dev/draftroom/backend/draftroom/valuation/candidates.py:2136) also reports healthy/non-injury overrides as vanished injury rows.

   Failure scenario: Marc records 12 games for a suspension with no designation. A later refresh marks the player IR. The detector skips him because an override exists, despite that override never addressing IR.

   Suppression should be limited to overrides that answer the current designation; at minimum, a stored nonempty designation mismatch should remain visible. `settled_by_override` should also be intersected with currently designated players.

4. **Malformed identity and audit fields are coerced instead of rejected.**  
   [playing_time.py](/C:/dev/draftroom/backend/draftroom/valuation/playing_time.py:263) checks emptiness before stripping, so `"   "` and `" null "` become accepted IDs. [playing_time.py](/C:/dev/draftroom/backend/draftroom/valuation/playing_time.py:287) turns `reason: null` and `date: null` into the nonempty string `"None"`.

   Failure scenario: a whitespace player ID silently becomes an unmatched warning, leaving the intended judgment unapplied. A null reason/date still applies a valuation change with an unusable audit trail.

   Validate normalized scalar IDs and require actual nonempty strings for `reason` and `date`. If `player_name` is supplied, consider failing on a clear on-board ID/name mismatch to catch a valid but mistargeted ID.

## Requested checks

- All internal callers of `_cap_expected_games_by_curve` were updated for tuple unpacking.
- Non-overridden behavior is unchanged, including `None` and zero.
- `ImpactEngine` reapplication is numerically idempotent, including zero and source-without-games cases.
- `RealBoard` has no external positional construction; `PoolPlayer`’s new field is safely appended after existing defaults.
- No nit-level findings.

I could not independently rerun pytest because this environment’s venv points to a missing Python 3.12 installation. The findings above come from tracing the actual working-tree call paths and constructors.
